APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
x 
Resolution 3577 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting connection to BOPU sanitary sewer services only for property outside City limits. 
This property is the Archer Complex, there is currently no existing sewer in the area or other outside user 
agreements. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is currently zoned a PUD Planned Unit Development in the County.  
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
The request for an outside user agreement has no formal review criteria; however, the following 
elements should be considered when reviewing outside user agreements: 
- 
BOPU capacity 
- 
Needed infrastructure improvements 
- 
Population served 
- 
Considerations on the effect on future annexation(s) 
- 
Fiscal cost/benefit to the City and the BOPU 
- 
Conformance with the Comprehensive Plan 
 
The requested outside user agreement has minimal impact on BOPU’s system capacity. The proposed 
use of the property includes light and medium density commercial and industrial facilities. The current 
existing facilities consist of a mix of administrative County owned facilities and the County Fair Grounds. 
The closest sewer connection is located at either Hutchins Drive or Venture Drive, which is on the north 
side of I-80 and to the West. This connection can be used or another connection can be proposed.

May 13, 2024 
Property tax would increase, but the City would not have any income from the property tax in the area. 
The BOPU may charge a higher rate for outside users. The sewer infrastructure will need to be extended 
to this area as it does not exist already, and Laramie County will be responsible for extending the 
infrastructure. City police, fire, and sanitation services are not within this area as it is solely within the 
County, so if this parcel were to come into City limits by itself, it would likely result in a need to extend 
the services to this area. 
 
The Comprehensive Plan designates this area as Mixed-use Employment – and the proposed use in this 
location is consistent with that anticipated development pattern. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is discussed above.    
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, the following comments were received: 
x 
BOPU has not received the OUA release form that is required for their approval. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application is acceptable based on the considerations specific to this request 
and therefore recommends approval of the outside user agreement.   
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the proposed outside user agreement.

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED:  "A RESOLUTION APPROVING THE BUDGET OF THE CHEYENNE 
REGIONAL AIRPORT BOARD FOR THE FISCAL YEAR BEGINNING 
JULY 1, 2024 AND ENDING JUNE 30, 2025." 
 
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Regional Airport Board ("Board") was created pursuant to 
Ordinance No. 2407 adopted by the Governing Body on February 11, 1980, as amended; 
 
WHEREAS, City Code Section 2.60.050(B) sets forth the requirement that the Board shall 
submit on or before the first day of May of each year, to the governing bodies of the city and the 
county, a proposed budget for the ensuing fiscal year; 
 
WHEREAS, City Code Section 2.60.050(B) provides that the Board budget shall be subject 
to alteration, modification, and approval by the Governing Body in accordance with the policies 
of the City; and 
 
WHEREAS, City staff has reviewed the proposed budget received from the Board. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the proposed budget for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 
2024 and ending June 30, 2025 of the Cheyenne Regional Airport Board, as set forth in the attached 
information with this Resolution entitled "Cheyenne Regional Airport Operating Budget for the 
Fiscal Year 2025" in the total amount of ten million eight hundred seven thousand fifty-five dollars 
and seventy-five cents ($10,807,055.75), is hereby approved. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
10:24 am, May 07 2024

 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Cheyenne Regional Airport 
Operating Budget FY 2025 
 

1 
 
Table of Contents

2

13 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

2 
 
Background 
The following pages present the Cheyenne Regional Airport Board’s operating budget 
for fiscal year 2025. The Board consists of five members, two members appointed by 
the City of Cheyenne, two members appointed by Laramie County, and one member 
appointed by the City and County in alternating terms. The Joint Powers Agreement 
sets forth the terms by which the City, County, and Airport operate. Under this 
agreement, the City and County annually review the Airport Budget. The City and 
County also sign all FAA grant agreements as co-sponsors. The Board operates 
under the authority of both an Airport Board under Wyoming State Statute and as a 
Joint Powers Board and holds ownership of all facilities, equipment, lease holdings, 
and operating rights. 
 
The Board adopted a Certificate of Organization on July 1, 1980, pursuant to the City 
of Cheyenne Ordinance 2407 and Laramie County Commissioners Resolution 
officially forming the Airport Board and electing officers under W.S.§ 10-4-101 through 
10-4-107. Under W.S.§ 16-1-104(d), the Airport Board was deemed a joint powers 
board.  
 
For the year January 1, 2024 – December 31, 2024, the slate of officers is: Kevin 
Paintner, President; Dr. Jeff Storey, Vice President; Melissa Amick, Treasurer; Kim 
Brokaw, Secretary; and Keith Zabka, Member.  
 
The Board’s fiscal year is from July 1 – June 30 each year. Once the Board approves 
the budget it is submitted to the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County for review by 
May 1st of each year.  
 
The Board operates the Airport as a business enterprise to be financially self-
sufficient. The Airport does not have the authority to tax and does not use local tax 
dollars, property tax or sales tax. The Airport is funded through fees paid by airport 
users, including airlines and businesses that operate at the airport.  
 
Community/Economic Impacts 
The airport is a key piece of infrastructure for our community, connecting citizens and 
employers to the country and globally. The airport supports not just passenger travel, 
but also the Wyoming Air National Guard and the State of Wyoming, among many 
other consumers. According to the Wyoming 2020 Aviation Economic Impact Study, 
Cheyenne Regional Airport’s commercial air services and airport activities provide 
over $27.5 million in annual economic activity to the community, including $4.98 
million for business tenants.  Wyoming airports provide over 20,000 jobs.   
Additionally, Wyoming airports have approximately 3,628 business tenants across the 
state.  Included in the amount are the approximate 60 tenants at the Cheyenne 
Regional Airport including: 
 
• Land leases to 21 various tenants including the Wyoming Air National Guard, 
the Wyoming Department of Transportation, and the City of Cheyenne.   
• Hangar rentals to 12 tenants including Alpine Air, the Flying School, and Reach 

3 
 
Air Medical Services. 
• Commercial leases to 29 tenants including various doctors, dentists, the US 
Postal Service, DFS, and WYDOT Aeronautics. 
 
Cheyenne Regional Airport Board Budget Summary 
Impacts 
Air Service 
The past few years have had a significant impact on the Airport’s air service.  During 
2020, we saw traveler numbers reduced due to the COVID-19 pandemic.  From 2020-
2023 we saw decreased travelers due to the runway reconstruction and several 
required full and partial runway shutdowns.  
 
In September 2023, the Airport’s final phase of the runway reconstruction was 
completed, and the Airport was able to return to full air service.  Even with no air 
service from April to September, the Airport saw 12,992 emplacements.  In 2022, there 
were 19,511 enplanements. Prior to the pandemic and runway reconstruction, there 
were 15,888 enplanements in 2019. At this time there is no anticipated future impact to 
air service.   
 
Summary 
This year we are presenting the budget with two enterprise centers. The enterprise 
centers include Airport Administration and Operations and Fixed Based Operations 
(FBO). Revenues, and expenses for the various enterprise centers and their 
associated cost centers are outlined in the following pages.  
 
Presented on pages 4-6 is a chart summarizing the Airport Board’s total budget for the 
fiscal year 2024 (July 1, 2024-June 30, 2025). We are expected to have net operating 
income of $340,362 with a net loss of $656,319 including all capital expenses.   
 
The blue color indicates that it is an amount received/paid by the Airport.  The orange 
color indicates amounts received/paid using other funds such as 6th penny funds, 
Wyoming Air National Guard, federal, and state grants.

Airport Administration and Operations  
The Airport’s operating budget is a stand-alone enterprise center separate from the 
FBO operations. All revenues generated from the operation of this enterprise center, 
including rents, commercial landing fees (other than FBO landing fees), and other 
operating fees, will be used to offset the expenses related to airport operations. This 
budget year the airport will use Airport reserves for operating/capital expenses in 
excess of revenues.  
 
The income (loss) from airport operations for FY 2025 is anticipated to be $268,491.   
The specific details for the airport administration and operations enterprise center are 
outlined on pages 7-10. The Airport’s capital plan can be found on page 13. 
Revenue 
Aeronautical revenues are collected from both the fixed base operator and the airlines 
as well as other aeronautical users of the airport. Non-aeronautical revenues are 
collected from a variety of sources including terminal concessions, rental cars, and 
lease revenue. These revenue streams are used to fund cost-center expenses. There 
are five revenue centers for airport administration and operations including air 
carriers, leased space, rental cars, concessions, and other income.  The total amount 
of revenue expected to be collected by Administration and Operations is $3,101,309.  A 
more detailed review of the anticipated revenues and sources for each of these profit 
centers is below. 
 
Air Carriers 
Air Carrier income is derived from three sources: landing fees, terminal rents, and 
Passenger Facility Charges (PFC). Landing fees and terminal rent revenues are used 
to offset expenses related to the airfield and terminal operations. PFC revenues are 
used to reimburse the Airport for approved projects previously completed.    
 
Due to the inconsistent air carrier income over the past several years, a conservative 
estimate is $170,664 for fiscal year 2025. Of this, $64,056 is terminal space rental, 
$25,044 is landing fees, and $81,564 are PFC’s.  
  
Leased Space 
The Airport currently has approximately 70 leases with various tenants including the 
Wyoming Department of Transportation (WYDOT), Wyoming Air National Guard 
(WANG), the City of Cheyenne, and the State of Wyoming. The leased space 
includes building rent, land rent, hangar rent (excluding community hangar), terminal 
rent (excluding rental cars and air carriers), and storage rent.   The leased space 
revenue is used to offset the expenses in the “Leased Space” cost center.   
 
The budget does include a 3% annual increase in rent for inflation but this may vary 
as we are unsure what the Consumer Price Index (CPI) will be during FY 2025.  
Leased space revenue for fiscal year 2025 is anticipated to be $2,364,337. 
 
Rental Cars 
Rental car revenue is predominantly composed of minimum annual guaranteed 
amounts (MAGs). The on-airport rental car companies are required to pay 10% of 

8 
 
gross revenue or their MAG, whichever is higher. This is included in the concession 
revenues below.   
 
The rental cars also pay rent for space in the terminal which is used to offset expenses 
in the “Terminal” cost center. The rental cars pay rent and utility costs for the use of 
the wash bay facility. These fees help to offset the cost of operating the wash bay 
which is included in the “Leased Space” cost center. The amount of rent revenue from 
rental cars for fiscal year 2025 is anticipated to be $8,226. 
 
The third type of rental car revenue is Customer Facility Charges (CFC) which are 
collected and held to offset rental car-related approved projects. This revenue is 
anticipated to be $94,976.   The rental car revenue for fiscal year 2025 is anticipated 
to be $103,202 excluding the concessions. 
 
Concessions 
Usually, most of the income from concessions is received from restaurant revenue. At 
this time, there is no active restaurant, so no restaurant concessions have been 
incorporated into this budget. Other concession revenue includes rental car 
concessions, ATM concessions, vending machine concessions, and ride-share 
concessions.   
 
All concession revenue is dependent on activity level and is hard to predict depending 
on market fluctuations. The concession revenue stream helps to fund the “Terminal” 
cost center. Revenue is budgeted to be $180,364 for fiscal year 2025. 
 
Other Income 
Other income includes interest income, advertising income, sixth penny sales tax (still 
being collected for parking lot) and other airport revenue such as badging fees. The 
budgeted amount for fiscal year 2025 is $282,742.

FY 25 ADMINISTRATION AND OPERATIONS REVENUE

9 
 
Expenses 
Budgeted expenses for airport administration and operations total $2,804,038. 
Operating expenses exclude FBO expenses and capital expenses as those are reflected 
in separate budgets on pages 11-13 following the detailed cost center information. 
 
The number of full-time equivalent staff for Airport administration and operations (non-
FBO) employees is currently 11 with two vacancies expected to be filled for this 
upcoming fiscal year 2025 and incorporated into the budget. Incorporated into the 
budget for both administration/operations and FBO is a 5% raise for all full-time 
employees which will ultimately vary from 2.5% to 5% depending on their annual 
employee evaluation.   Finally, there is a 5% adjustment, for both 
administration/operations and FBO, for employer-provided benefits excluding health 
insurance which is not anticipated to increase for FY 25 unless employees change 
from their current selection (i.e. individual or family) in the course of the year. 
 
Also incorporated into this budget is an increase in insurance premiums, for both 
administration/operations and FBO.  Per discussions with our insurance company, 
rates in the area are expected to increase 25-30% for FY 25.  We increased our 
budget for employee training and IT hardware to incorporate the purchase of new 
computers that are hitting their life cycle.  Finally, we increased the budget for 
repairs/maintenance and supplies as aging infrastructure requires additional repairs.   
 
There are five direct cost centers for the Airport: Administration, airfield, leased space, 
other buildings and grounds, and terminal. There are 16 corresponding expense 
categories. The expense categories include areas such as personnel expenses, 
utilities and building expenses, operations and maintenance expenses, and 
equipment expenses. 
 
Administration and Operations Cost Center 
This cost center includes all administrative and operations expenses that were not 
specifically assigned to another cost center.  This also includes expenses related to 
administrative and operations staff expenses.   
 
Airfield Cost Center 
This cost center includes the costs associated with the airfield and air carrier apron 
including snow removal, aircraft rescue firefighting, operations and maintenance 
expenses, and utilities expenses. This cost center is offset by revenues collected by 
Air Carriers.   
 
Leased Space Cost Center 
This cost center includes all expenses related to our leased property.  This cost 
center also includes the airport-owned hangars, the rental car wash bays as well as 
aeronautical and non-aeronautical tenants. This cost center is offset by revenues 
collected by the Rental Cars and Leased Space revenue centers.   
 
Terminal 
Included in the terminal cost center are all costs associated with the terminal building 

10 
 
including repair and maintenance, custodial, utilities, the parking lot, baggage system 
and certain security items related to access control (cameras, doors, alarms). This 
cost center is offset by revenues collected by Air Carriers, Rental Cars, and 
Concessions revenue centers.  
 
Other Buildings and Grounds 
This cost center includes the expenses associated other grounds at the airport 
including the parks and grassy areas as well as roadways at the Airport.

anticipated that for FY 2025, the FBO would have a total net income of $223,272.

12 
 
Revenues 
The FBO has the following revenue centers: Fuel sales (Skywest, Military, Contract, 
Other), FBO services, and miscellaneous revenue. 
 
Revenues for the FBO are hard to predict due to the past several years being 
inconsistent.  For a conservative estimate, we averaged FY22 and FY23 revenue for 
FBO Services and Misc. Revenue.   
 
Fuel revenues were estimated based on partial FY 23 and FY 22 sales due to our 
runway closure in FY 22, and factored in a 10% decrease due to trends across the 
nation for FBOs.   
 
Expenses 
The FBO has the same cost centers as the Administration and Operations enterprise 
center. The FBO has seven full-time employees and three part-time employees with 
an additional part-time employee anticipated to be hired in the next few months.  Fuel 
costs are based on a combination of FY 23 and FY 22 expenses and are decreased 
by 10% decrease due to trends across the nation for FBOs. Unfortunately, this is 
extremely hard to predict as we are unsure what fuel prices will be in the upcoming 
year.   
 

13 
 
 
Airport Capital FY 2025 Budget 
Capital expenses such as airfield equipment purchases and airfield construction costs 
are typically funded through the Capital Improvement Program (CIP). Capital funds 
include the Federal Airport Improvement Program (AIP), state grant funds, 6th penny 
funds, Passenger Facility Charges (PFCs), and rental car Customer Facility Charges 
(CFCs).  All the proposed projects for FY 2025 are detailed below.   
 
   
Cash Balance Forecast 
As of March 31, 2024, the Board has $2,883,244 in cash reserves.  Of that, $874,377 
is currently restricted in investments and unavailable until October 2025.  As indicated 
in the overall budget on page 6, approximately $656,319 will need to come from 
reserves or debt services. Currently, the Airport has no outstanding debt service. Of 
that amount, $86,222 is required to be paid for capital projects for our local match.  
 
If using available cash reserves, the projected remaining cash balance at the end of 
FY 2025 would be $1,352,548.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-05-14_TotalExtraction_15197387.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Charles W. Bloom, AICP, Planning and Development Director  
Subject: Downtown Development Authority Proposed Budget - Fiscal Year 2025 
Date: 
May 13, 2025 
 
Background:  
 
Attached is the proposed budget for fiscal year 2025 (July 1 to June 30). The proposed Governing Body 
review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral: May 13, 2025 
• 
Public Services Committee: May 20, 2025 
• 
Final consideration: May 28, 2025 
 
The budget represents a $76.649.16 increase in mill levy assessment collections ($463,649.16) and an 
increase in interest earned. The interest earned estimate is based on an estimated $1 million dollars 
being left in the Wyoming Government Investment Fund (WGIF) which as of April 12, 2024, has a seven-
day yield of 5.19%. 
 
The proposed budget is a decrease in the budget from the prior year. The proposed budget of 
$1,049,049 is $187,101 less than fiscal year 2024, brings in $500,000 from reserves, accounts for a 
$20,000 grant from the Cheyenne-Laramie Co. Economic Development Joint Powers Board, and $5,000 
in funding from Laramie County. The City contributes $209,929 covering all payroll, insurance, and 
benefits for employees. Other City staff also support DDA employees at no cost to the DDA. 
 
Highlights of the budget are below.   
 
• 
Visit Cheyenne sponsored events: 
o 
July, Downtown Express Shuttle, $7,000.00 
o 
July, Cheyenne Day, $5,000.00 
o 
September, OktoberFest/Comm App Day, $10,000.00 
o 
October, Downtown Halloween, $4,700.00 
o 
December, Old West Holiday, $15,000.00 
o 
December, Running of the Santas, $1,800.00 
o 
December, New Year's Eve, $5,000.00 
o 
April, Tales & Ales Pub Crawl, $1,500.00 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

 
• 
DDA Board Strategic Projects 
This line item replaces the previous “Large Projects” line item and provides clarity that this is a 
special fund to carry our unique Board projects.  
 
• 
Sponsorship 
This line item represents sponsorships to other events and organizations that may make 
requests throughout the year. Allocations will be made on a request basis and sponsorship 
funds must face public facing activities and programs that have a direct public benefit.  
 
• 
Downtown Planter Program  
This program has been increased based on discussions with potential providers.   
 
• 
Façade Improvement Program (FIP) 
This is a preservation-based facade improvement program aimed at revitalizing historical 
structures by restoring and enhancing their exterior appearance while fostering economic 
growth through increased property values and tourism. The program has not been used in that 
capacity recently and staff intends to further develop the program in the upcoming year. For 
budgeting and accounting purposes, we recommend that this and the Capital Improvement 
Grant program be independent line items.     
 
• 
Capital Improvement Grant (CIG) Program  
This program has been commonly used in recent years and is comprised of a majority of the 
grants awarded in recent years. In fiscal year 2024, $500,000 was budgeted and it is expected 
that all grants will be allocated prior to the end of the fiscal year. For budgeting and accounting 
purposes, we recommend that this and the Façade Improvement Program be independent line 
items.     
 
• 
Sidewalk Match Program 
This is a new line item to carry out the “Sidewalk Repair Assistance Program” which will be in 
response to the City Engineering Department’s sidewalk repair initiative.  
 
• 
Logo Design Program 
Staff recommends zeroing out this program because logo and brand development results in a 
copyrighted product exclusive to a private party. Additionally, a similar program exists through 
the Wyoming Small Business Development Center Network which offers logo design assistance. 
 
The DDA Board may elect to adopt the budget as presented or make modifications. If the DDA Board 
desires more discussion on this item, this can be postponed to a special meeting next week. 
 
The DDA Board approved this budget at their April 18, 2024. 
 
Attachments: 
1. Resolution 

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION APPROVING THE BUDGET OF THE CHEYENNE 
DOWNTOWN DEVELOPMENT AUTHORITY FOR THE FISCAL YEAR 
BEGINNING JULY 1, 2024 AND ENDING JUNE 30, 2025." 
 
WHEREAS, the Downtown Development Authority ("DDA") was created pursuant to 
Ordinance No. 2804 adopted by the Governing Body on July 9, 1984; 
 
WHEREAS, Wyoming Statute 15-9-208(a) requires the DDA to propose a budget, which 
shall be administratively reviewed by the City prior to submission of the proposed budget to the 
Governing Body for approval; 
 
WHEREAS, Cheyenne City Code, Chapter 2.76.050(C), provides that the DDA budget 
shall be subject to alteration, modification and approval by the Governing Body in accordance 
with the policies of the City; 
 
WHEREAS, the DDA Board approved the proposed budget at their April 18, 2024, 
meeting; and 
 
WHEREAS, City staff has reviewed the proposed budget received from the DDA .  
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the proposed budget for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 
2024 and ending June 30, 2025 of the Downtown Development Authority, as set forth in the 
attached information with this Resolution entitled "Downtown Development Authority Proposed 
Budget - Fiscal Year 2025" in the total amount of $1,049,049.00, is hereby approved.

MEMO 
 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins, City of Cheyenne 
 
 
City Council Members 
 
FROM:  
Cassie L. Pickett, PE, Engineering Manager 
 
DATE:  
April 17, 2024 
 
SUBJECT: 
Dutch Brothers - Improvement Agreement  
 
Purpose of Project: 
This project is the private development of land located at the southwest corner of Pershing Blvd and 
McCann Ave for the purpose of a Dutch Brothers.  
 
Reason for Contract Modification:  
This agreement was the result of the Dutch Brothers Development Traffic Impact Study noting the 
need for a right turn lane on Pershing. Dutch Brothers requested to submit cash-in-lieu rather than 
building the turn lane. This is represented within the referenced agreement.   
 
Estimated Cost: 
The total contract amount is $70,500.00.   The funding location will be GL#001-00-0000-20-00613.     
The Engineer’s Department has reviewed the request and respectfully recommends approval of the 
requested Public Improvements Agreement. 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
CITY ENGINEER’S OFFICE 
2101 O’Neil Avenue  
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001

IMPROVEMENTS AGREEMENT  
 
 
THIS AGREEMENT made and entered into this  day of  
 , 2024, by and 
between DB Cheyenne, LLC, a Colorado limited liability company (the "Owner"), Elevations Credit 
Union  (the "Holder of a Deed of Trust"), and the City of Cheyenne, a municipal corporation existing 
under the laws of the State of Wyoming("City"). 
 
WITNESSETH: 
 
WHEREAS, “Cash-in-lieu” shall mean those payments made by Owner in advance 
pursuant to UDC Sections 4.1.5.b.6 and 4.1.5.b.7 to satisfy in full Owner’s, Holder of a Deed 
of Trust, or the Owner obligations with respect to certain specified Public Improvements as set 
forth herein.   
 
WHEREAS, the Owner has made proper application and supporting document(s) for request 
(Exemption from Platting OR Site Development Plan), Engineering Plan Review (PUDC) Number 
PUDC-23-75 (the "Case") for the property described in the Case (the "Property"); 
 
WHEREAS, as a condition of approval of the Case, the Owner is obligated to enter into an 
agreement with the City that provides that the Owner shall either construct certain public 
improvements set forth on Exhibit A attached hereto and incorporated herein (collective, the 
"Improvements") or provide a cash-in-lieu Payments of the same (the "Guarantee");  
 
WHEREAS, Cashin-lieu Amounts and Financial Security for Public Improvements.  
Upon Owner’s advance payment of Owner’s proportionate fair share of Public Improvements 
in accordance with UDC Sections 4.1.5.b.6 and 4.1.5.b.7, which payment amounts (the "Cash-
in-lieu Amounts") shall be as specified in Exhibit B, Owner’s financial responsibility for such 
scheduled Public Improvements shall be deemed permanently satisfied with respect to 
construction on the Property. The parties agree that the cash-in-lieu Amounts may be utilized 
by the City for construction of Public Improvements to the area with no limitation on time of 
completion, except as otherwise specified or required under existing statute, law, code, or 
ordinance.  Furthermore, upon notice to the City, the Owner may construct Public Improvements 
specified in Exhibit A in advance of City completion.  The City shall reimburse the Owner for 
accepted improvements at costs identified in Exhibit B. The Financial Security shall be eligible 
for release upon request by the Owner after inspection and acceptance of installed Public 
Improvements by the City; 
 
WHEREAS, The Parties agree to the installation of those Public Improvements, 
associated cost estimates and fee schedules, and dedications or Cash-in-lieu payments for Public 
Improvement Construction set forth in Exhibit B.  The Parties agree that the specifications for the 
Public Improvements delineated in Exhibit A and B (including without limitation) timing of 
3:35 pm, Apr 16 2024

 
Improvements Agreement 
Page 2 of 5 
commencement or completion, specific locations of the easements and points of beginning/points of 
termination of easements, roads and water or sanitary sewer lines, road width, and/or water or 
sanitary sewer line sizes, may be administratively modified by separate agreement of both Parties 
without the requirement of Cheyenne City Council hearings or approvals.  Administrative approval 
of such modifications shall not be unreasonably withheld, conditioned, or delayed; and 
 
WHEREAS, the parties desire to enter into this Agreement to satisfy the above condition of 
approval. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, for and in consideration of the mutual promises, covenants, and 
agreements herein set forth, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. 
The Owner shall construct and install, at its sole expense, all the Improvements, 
except any improvements identified on Exhibit A as Cash-in-lieu payments.  The Owner shall pay to 
the City Cash-in lieu payments in the amount set forth on Exhibit A, if any. Such cash- in-lieu shall 
be paid prior to the issuance of any Certificates of Occupancy. 
 
2. 
All Improvements shall be constructed in compliance with the following: 
 
a. 
All documents submitted prior to or at the time of approval of the Case;

All laws of the United States, State of Wyoming, and the City and its various 
agencies, affected special districts and/or servicing authorities; and

Such other designs, drawings, maps, specifications, sketches, and other 
matter submitted to and approved by any of the above-stated governmental entities. 
 
3. 
To secure and guarantee performance of its obligations as set forth herein, the Owner 
agrees that there shall be no conveyance, sale, or transfer of title of all or any portion of the Property 
nor shall certificate of occupancy be issued until improvements and/or cash-in-lieu has been received 
and approved by the City certifying that the Owner has satisfied any and all terms, conditions, notes, 
and restrictions set forth on the mylar or any supporting document(s) approved as part of the Case 
that must be completed by the Owner prior to the issuance of a Certificate of Occupancy, including, 
without limitation the satisfactory completion of the Improvements or the posting of a Guarantee, or 
cash-in-lieu for such completion. 
 
4. 
The Owner and Holder of a Deed of Trust hereby consent and authorize the City to 
enter onto the Property to construct, install, maintain, and/or replace all or any portion of the 
Improvements required by the Case. No Guarantee will be accepted for Improvements that lie outside 
of the Property unless the property owner grants an acceptable easement to the City. The Owner and 
the Holder of a Deed of Trust hereby unconditionally waive and release the City from any and all 
claims or other legal liability that arise out of or are attributable to constructing, installing, 
maintaining and/or replacing the Improvements. This consent, waiver and release extends to the City

Open Space Approach
Per UDC §6.7.4., Open Space is required at 5% of the building
footprint or a minimum of 47 SF. A covered outdoor seating area is
proposed north of the building in conjunction with the walk-up service
window. Outdoor furniture, seating, and/or decorative paving may be
used to define the space. Other amenities may be installed at the
Owner's discretion without amending this Site Plan.

Notes
1.
Base Information: Boundary and base information, including plat
information, is per Steil Surveying. Underground utilities as shown are
not guaranteed to be accurate. Off-site base information, including
lane stripes in Pershing Blvd. and curb & gutter on opposite sides of
McCann & Pershing were digitized from aerial photography.
2.
Ownership & Maintenance: Site improvements shown on this
drawing are to be owned and maintained by the Owner or Assigns.
3.
Architecture. Restaurant building is per R4 Architects, 2023. See
Building Elevations for compliance with UDC Section 6.7, trash
enclosure design, and height warning bar.
4.
Grading, Drainage, & Utilities: Grading, drainage, and utility design
provided by Lovas Engineering, P.C., may be adjusted during the
construction permitting process following approval of this Site Plan
without necessitating its revision. Refer to Building Mechanical
drawings for wet utility services entering buildings.
5.
Vehicular Access: The existing access at McCann Avenue is
proposed to remain, except as modified above. No additional right of
way dedication is proposed. Vehicular access through the property
will be permitted as required when Lots 9 & 10 are improved.
6.
Lighting: Light locations shown are preliminary, pending updated
lighting design by lighting & electrical consultant. Lighting plans will be
submitted with the building permit application and will comply with
UDC Section 6.4.
7.
Parking: Parking is reserved for employees and patrons. Parking may
be signed as "Reserved" or "Employees Only" at Owner discretion
without amending this plan.
8.
Trash Vehicle Access: Trash vehicles may enter the property from
McCann, access the trash enclosure for front-loader use, back up,
and exit the property to E. Pershing Blvd. via the 32' width mutual
access easement gravel drive while facing forward.

Date:
LANDSCAPE NOTES
1.
ALL PLANT MATERIALS SHALL BE IN ACCORDANCE WITH ANA SPECIFICATIONS
FOR NUMBER ONE GRADE.
2. IRRIGATED TURF AND NATIVE SEED AREAS:
IRRIGATED TURF AREAS. SOD WITH KORBY SOD "XERISCAPE TURF
BLEND" OR APPROVED EQUAL. (CONTAINS 80% TALL FESCUE AND 20%
KENTUCKY BLUEGRASS).
NATIVE SEED DRYLAND MIX. REFER TO "PRE-APPROVED DRYLAND MIX"
PER UDC APPENDIX G-8. MOW NATIVE SEEDED AREAS SEASONALLY TO A
HEIGHT OF 6" FOLLOWING ESTABLISHMENT. DO NOT ALLOW TYPICAL
HEIGHT TO EXCEED 12" IN R.O.W. AND 18" ON PRIVATE PROPERTY.
3. ALL SHRUB BEDS SHALL BE IRRIGATED WITH AUTOMATIC DRIP IRRIGATION
SYSTEM OR ACCEPTABLE ALTERNATIVE. THE IRRIGATION SYSTEM IS TO BE
ADJUSTED TO MEET THE NEEDS OF THE INDIVIDUAL PLANT MATERIAL.
GENERALLY, TREES PLANNED FOR DRIP IRRIGATION SHOULD HAVE A
MINIMUM OF 2 EMITTERS OR BUBBLERS PLACED ON OPPOSITE SIDES OF THE
STEM OR DRIP TUBING RINGS (PREFERRED) WITH A MINIMUM OF 4 INLINE
EMITTERS. ALL DRIP IRRIGATION SHOULD BE PLACED ON TOP OF THE ROOT
BALL. EACH TREE SHOULD BE WATERED TWICE PER WEEK AT A RATE OF 20
GALLONS PER CYCLE DURING THE GROWING SEASON AND ONCE PER MONTH
AT A RATE OF 20-30 GALLONS DURING THE DORMANT SEASON. HAND
WATERING WILL BE NECESSARY DURING THE DORMANT SEASON. SHRUBS,
PERENNIALS AND ORNAMENTAL GRASSES SHOULD HAVE 1-2 EMITTERS OR
BUBBLERS PER PLANT AND SHOULD BE WATERED TWICE PER WEEK AT A
RATE OF 5-10 GALLONS PER WATERING.
TREES PLANTED IN DRYLAND SEEDED AREAS SHALL RECEIVE DRIP
IRRIGATION AS PER ABOVE.
4. THE LANDSCAPE IRRIGATION SYSTEM MUST BE APPROVED BY THE CITY OF
CHEYENNE PRIOR TO THE ISSUANCE OF A BUILDING PERMIT.
5. ALL TREES SHALL BE BALLED & BURLAPPED IN A ROOT CONTROL BAG OR
CONTAINERIZED. INSTALL PER DETAIL THIS SHEET. COORDINATE
INSTALLATION ON ENGINEERED SLOPES WITH CIVIL ENGINEER. IN THE EVENT
OF A CONFLICT, NOTIFY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT.
6. ALL SHRUB BEDS SHALL RECEIVE 3/4" DIAMETER (MINIMUM) ROUNDED RIVER
ROCK COBBLE MULCH (ASSORTED COLORS) OR APPROVED EQUAL TO A
MINIMUM DEPTH OF 3" AT POST-COMPACTION ABOVE TYPAR 3301 (3 OZ.) OR
APPROVED EQUAL WEED BARRIER FILTER FABRIC. COBBLE SIZE MAY BE
INCREASED, AND / OR SHREDDED WESTERN CEDAR ("GORILLA HAIR") MAY BE
SUBSTITUTED, AT OWNER'S SOLE DISCRETION. PLACE GORILLA HAIR WITHIN
6" OF DISH OF TREES, SHRUBS,  ORNAMENTAL GRASSES, AND PERENNIALS.
6. ALL SHRUB BEDS SHALL RECEIVE 0.75" DIAMETER (MINIMUM) ROUNDED
RIVER ROCK COBBLE MULCH (ASSORTED COLORS) OR APPROVED EQUAL TO
A MINIMUM DEPTH OF 3" AT POST-SETTLING ABOVE TYPAR 3301 (3 OZ.) OR
APPROVED EQUAL WEED BARRIER FILTER FABRIC (NOT IMPERMEABLE
PLASTIC SHEETING). DO NOT COMPACT MULCH. COBBLE SIZE MAY BE
INCREASED AT THE OWNER'S SOLE DISCRETION.
7. SEPARATE ALL AREAS RECEIVING MULCH FROM IRRIGATED TURF AND
NATIVE TURF AREAS WITH 6"  ROLLTOP STEEL EDGING OR APPROVED EQUAL.
8. LOCATE ALL UTILITIES PRIOR TO PLANTING.
9. INSTALL TREES SUCH THAT ROOT PITS ARE OUTSIDE OF WATER AND
SANITARY SEWER EASEMENTS, A MINIMUM OF 10' CLEAR OF WATER AND
SEWER LINES, AND 4' CLEAR OF GAS AND STORM SEWER LINES. INSTALL
SHRUBS SUCH THAT ROOT PITS ARE 4' CLEAR OF WATER AND SANITARY
SEWER LINES AND NOT DIRECTLY ABOVE STORM SEWER LINES.
10. TREE UTILITY SEPARATIONS SHALL NOT BE USED AS A MEANS OF AVOIDING
THE INSTALLATION OF PLANT MATERIALS SHOWN ON THIS PLAN. DEVELOPER
SHALL ENSURE THAT THE LANDSCAPE PLAN IS COORDINATED WITH THE
PLANS DONE BY OTHER CONSULTANTS SO THE PROPOSED GRADING, STORM
DRAINAGE, OR OTHER CONSTRUCTION NEITHER CONFLICT WITH, NOR
PRECLUDE INSTALLATION AND MAINTENANCE OF, LANDSCAPE ELEMENTS
SHOWN.
11. THE LANDSCAPE INSTALLATION CONTRACTOR IS RESPONSIBLE FOR ALL SOIL
TESTING REQUIRED.
12. THE SOIL IN ALL LANDSCAPE AREAS SHALL BE THOROUGHLY LOOSENED TO
A DEPTH OF NOT LESS THAN EIGHT INCHES (8") BELOW FINAL GRADE.
AMENDMENTS SHALL BE THOROUGHLY INCORPORATED INTO THE SOIL OF
ALL LANDSCAPE AREAS TO ACHIEVE A MINIMUM 3% ORGANIC MATERIAL AND
MAXIMUM SALINITY LEVEL OF 1.0 MILLIOHMS PER CENTIMETER IN THE TOP
SIX INCHES (6") OF THE SOIL PROFILE (CHEYENNE UDC 6.3.7.b).
13. TO THE MAXIMUM EXTENT FEASIBLE, TOPSOIL REMOVED FOR
CONSTRUCTION ACTIVITY SHALL BE CONSERVED FOR LATER USE ON AREAS
REQUIRING LANDSCAPING.
14. MINOR CHANGES IN SPECIES AND PLANT LOCATIONS MAY BE MADE DURING
CONSTRUCTION, AS REQUIRED BY SITE CONDITIONS OR PLANT AVAILABILITY.
OVERALL QUANTITY, QUALITY, AND DESIGN CONCEPT SHALL REMAIN
CONSISTENT WITH APPROVED PLANS. IN THE EVENT OF CONFLICT WITH THE
QUANTITIES INCLUDED IN THE PLANT LIST, SPECIES AND QUANTITIES
ILLUSTRATED ON THIS PLAN SHALL PREVAIL AND MUST BE PRE-APPROVED
BY THE CITY'S DEVELOPMENT OFFICE.
15. LANDSCAPE AND IRRIGATION SHALL BE INSTALLED PRIOR TO CERTIFICATE
OF OCCUPANCY. TO SECURE A CONDITION OF OCCUPANCY WITHOUT
LANDSCAPE AND IRRIGATION FULLY INSTALLED, REMAINING LANDSCAPE AND
IRRIGATION TO BE INSTALLED SHALL BE SECURED WITH WITH AN
ACCEPTABLE FORM OF SURETY TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE FOR 125% OF
THE VALUE OF THE LANDSCAPE PRIOR TO CERTIFICATE OF OCCUPANCY.
16. THE APPLICANT, SUCCESSORS, AND ASSIGNS SHALL BE JOINTLY AND
SEVERALLY RESPONSIBLE FOR THE REGULAR AND NORMAL MAINTENANCE
OF ALL LANDSCAPE ELEMENTS SHOWN ON THIS PLAN. LANDSCAPE SHALL BE
MAINTAINED FREE OF DISEASE, PESTS, WEEDS, AND LITTER. MULCH AND
WEED BARRIER FABRIC SHALL BE REPLACED AS NECESSARY. LANDSCAPE
ELEMENTS SUCH AS FENCES AND WALLS SHALL BE REPAIRED AND/OR
REPLACED TO REMAIN STRUCTURALLY SOUND.
17. PLANT MATERIALS EXHIBITING EVIDENCE OF INSECTS, DISEASE, AND OTHER
DAMAGE SHALL BE APPROPRIATELY TREATED. DEAD TREES, SHRUBS, AND
PERENNIAL PLANTS SHALL BE REPLACED WITHIN 30 DAYS IF POSSIBLE, OR
BY JUNE 1 OF THE FOLLOWING GROWING SEASON.
18. LEAVE PLANT IDENTIFICATION TAGS ON PLANT MATERIAL, TO BE REMOVED
BY CITY FOLLOWING INSPECTION.

DUTCH BROS COFFEE – DECEL LANE ESTIMATE (PUDC-23-75)
12/6/2023
Dear Mrs. Pickett,
In response to comment from the City Engineering Dept. during the EPR process for the above 
referenced project, below is a list of bid items for probable construction costs. 
*It should be noted that opinions of probable construction costs rendered by Engineer represent our 
reasonable professional engineering opinion and furnished for general guidance.  Engineer makes no 
representation, warranty, or guarantee, expressed nor implied, as to the accuracy of such opinions as 
compared to bid or actual costs.
Please note that civil design for the deceleration lane has not been completed at this time.  The 
estimated quantities for civil infrastructure are based upon planimetric design by Palma Land Planning.  
Lovas Engineering, PC opinion of approximate construction costs have been derived using the 
following assumed key parameters:
•
6” asphalt over 8” Grading ‘W’ for all asphalt pavement (3” grading ‘A’ / 3” grading ’D’)
•
277 LF type ‘A’ curb & gutter
•
275 LF saw cutting
•
110 CY unclassified excavation
•
(1) 20’ radius curb fillet
•
(1) concrete ADA ramp with detectable warning plates
The estimate also includes estimated contractor fees such as bonds/insurance, mobilization, potholing 
utilities, force account, traffic control, stormwater management and construction management.  The 
estimate is based on present value and does not account for inflation in construction materials or labor 
costs.  The estimate does not include engineering, planning or consulting fees.  
Engineer’s Opinion of Approximate Construction Costs: 
$47,000.00

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
April 17, 2024 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Univerus, Inc., 
for the Sanitation Rerouting and Optimization Project 
 ______________________________________________ 
One (1) Responsive Proposal was submitted under Request for Proposal (RFP) #4-24, for the 
Sanitation Rerouting and Optimization Project.  
  
The proposal was received from:  
1. Univerus, Inc. 
 
Upon review of the one (1) responsive proposal by the evaluation panel, the group has 
recommended acceptance of the proposal submitted by Univerus, Inc., for a project award amount 
of $142,676.00. 
 
Basis for award was based on a Qualifications and Cost based review and scoring of proposals. 
 
Funding:   
 (Solid Waste Fund / Professional Services) 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 4-24 
Sanitation Rerouting and Optimization Project  
Page 1 of 7 
City Contract No. ____________ 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for 
SANITATION REROUTING AND OPTIMIZATION PROJECT 
 
This Professional Services Agreement (“Agreement”) is made and entered into by and 
between the City of Cheyenne, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
(“City”), and Univerus, Inc., located at 514 Kennett Pike, Chadds Ford, Pennsylvania 19317 
(“Consultant”).  This Agreement shall become effective upon the date of the last signature 
affixed hereto. 
 
RECITALS 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by 
and between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the Sanitation Rerouting and 
Optimization Project, in accordance with City of Cheyenne Request for Proposal 
(RFP) #4-24, incorporated herein by reference, and the Consultant’s submitted 
proposal, incorporated herein by reference. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 
The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed by January 31, 2025. 
 
3. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the 
Consultant will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant a not to exceed amount 
of One Hundred Forty-Two Thousand, Six Hundred Seventy-Six Dollars 
($142,676.00) as stated in Exhibit A – Price Proposal. 
 
4. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances. Upon notice to the 
Consultant, the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services 
not meeting such a standard. 
 
5. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement 
in accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
 
 
 
11:58 am, Apr 17 2024

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 4-24 
Sanitation Rerouting and Optimization Project  
Page 2 of 7 
6. 
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug-Free Workplace Act of November 1988, the City of 
Cheyenne has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to 
alcohol and drug usage by City Employees. All architects, engineers, and other 
consultants under contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and 
sub-consultants, are required to comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and 
Controlled Substance Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites 
occupied by the Consultant while performing the duties and responsibilities of the 
Agreement. It is the responsibility of the Consultant to familiarize themselves with the 
requirements of this policy and to inform all their employees and sub-consultants of the 
requirements and ensure their compliance. If the Consultant, their employees, or 
sub-consultants are found in violation of this policy, the Agreement may be terminated. 
 
7. 
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS 
The Consultant agrees to indemnify, hold harmless, and defend the City from and against 
any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures, and suits, and the cost and expenses 
incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a 
result the performance of the Consultant’s duties, including death or bodily injury to any 
person, destruction or damage to any property, contamination of or adverse effects on the 
environment, or any violation of governmental laws, regulations, or orders to the extent 
caused by (1) the Consultant’s breach of any term or provision of this Agreement; or (2) 
any negligent or wrongful act, error, or omission by the Consultant, or its employees or 
sub-consultants in the performance of this Agreement.  The Consultant acknowledges 
that it may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant to the terms of this paragraph. 
 
8. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Consultant must provide proof of the following insurance coverages: 
 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness, or death, or from damage to or destruction 
of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of $1,000,000 
per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the Agreement, 
as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services under this 
Agreement.  The consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’ compensation 
or employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
 
Professional Liability Insurance 
The Consultant shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City from 
any and all claims arising from the Consultant’s negligence in the performance of duties 
under this Agreement.  The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be 
provided pursuant to an “occurrence” policy. 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 4-24 
Sanitation Rerouting and Optimization Project  
Page 3 of 7 
  
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
 
 
a. 
Consultant shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, 
provide the City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such 
coverage is or shall be in effect at the time the Consultant begins the 
provision of services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
b. 
In the event the Consultant’s services extend into a future policy period, 
the Consultant shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City 
with a new certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or 
shall be in effect during all periods of time that Consultant will provide 
services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
c. 
Consultant shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five 
(5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services 
under this Agreement; and 
 
 
d. 
In the event the Consultant or the insurer terminates “claims made” 
coverage prior to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs 
a., b., or c. of this paragraph, the Consultant shall provide to the City 
advance written notification of the termination of said coverage and shall 
provide the City with an endorsement for an extended reporting period 
(“tail coverage”) which shall be in effect for a period of time not less than 
five (5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services 
under this Agreement.  
 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
 
The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by 
endorsement on its insurance policies and shall provide the City with a copy of the 
endorsements.  This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and 
professional liability insurance policies. 
 
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the 
above-stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All 
policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the 
Agreement.  It shall be an affirmative obligation upon Consultant to immediately notify 
in writing the city risk manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or 
occurrence that has resulted in or may result in the cancellation of, or substantive change 
to any insurance coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be 
construed to be a breach of this Agreement. 
 
If requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of insurance 
policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional insured.  
The City’s failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall 
not affect the City’s rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder. 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 4-24 
Sanitation Rerouting and Optimization Project  
Page 4 of 7 
 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a minimum 
A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
9. 
MINORITY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES 49 C.F.R. Part 26 
All parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in, 
denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award 
and performance of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national 
origin, or sex. 
 
10. 
MONTHLY REPORT 
With every monthly billing, the Consultant shall provide the City Representative with a 
written statement of the status of the work with respect to the Scope of Work, time sheets, 
and work schedule. Failure to provide the required monthly report will delay processing 
of any payment request until the report is submitted. 
 
11. 
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT 
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this 
Agreement. The Consultant shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities 
that may be incurred by the Consultant in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing 
in this Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or 
employees to act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City or to incur any 
obligation of any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
12. 
TAXES 
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, 
debts, and obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this 
Agreement. 
 
13. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
14. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default.  In the event the default 
remains uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
 
 
a. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not 
exceed the contract amount; or 
 
 
b. 
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
 
 
 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 4-24 
Sanitation Rerouting and Optimization Project  
Page 5 of 7 
15. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
The term of this Agreement shall be from date of execution to January 31, 2025.   
 
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Consultant, 
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. 
Upon receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall: 
 
 
a. 
Discontinue all services affected; and 
 
 
b. 
Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications, 
reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as 
may have been accumulated by the Consultant in performing this 
Agreement, whether completed or in process. 
 
 
c. 
In the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the 
Consultant for accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of 
termination. Such acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
 
16. 
ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, 
the City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Consultant. In the event of 
default of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default 
to commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear 
its own costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.  
 
17. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by 
the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have 
jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the 
venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
18. 
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and employees, does not waive its 
governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary 
for the parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of the 
Agreement.  Furthermore, the City specifically retains all immunities and defenses 
available to it as a sovereign or governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et 
seq., and all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law, 
enforcement actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a waiver of 
governmental immunity. The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not 
be strictly construed, either against or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to 
governmental or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental 
immunity. 
 
19. 
MONITORING ACTIVITIES 
The City shall have the right to monitor all activities related to this Agreement that are 
performed by the Consultant or its sub-consultants. This shall include, but not be limited 
to, the right to make site inspections at any time and with reasonable notice; to bring

COST OF WORK 
Cover Letter 
Univerus Inc, and its product Univerus Routing (Formerly known as C2Logix) are proud to respond 
to this RFP, as we feel strongly that we can meet and exceed the requirements set forth in the 
document. 
Univerus Routing will celebrate its 24th anniversary in January 2024.  During those 24 years we 
have seen companies in our industry come and go.  In short, we feel that we have been able to 
succeed over the years by sticking to what we know best, Route Optimization Solutions, catering to a 
select few industries and applications, and treating our customers the way we hope to be treated.   
Early in 2020 C2logix merged with Univerus Inc, and rebranded Univerus Routing.  Univerus is a 
conglomeration of software companies that cater mainly to government organizations with a 
majority of its clients being municipal entities.  Even as we work to meld with the other Univerus 
solutions into our Unity Suite we still function autonomously as Univerus Routing, and continue to 
work with clients as an independent solution, now with the backing and support of a larger 
corporation. 
Univerus Routing has been involved with many projects like the one described in the Cheyenne RFP 
document.  We feel that our solutions provide a special differentiation to other potential bidders to 
this RFP.  In the past 18 months, our team has worked with the solid waste optimizations of 
Baltimore, (Maryland), Atlanta, (Georgia), Concord, (North Carolina), Glendale, (Arizona), Scranton, 
(Pennsylvania), and more.  
Additionally, we are existing partners with FleetMind.  We have provided similar services to 
those requested in this RFP for Clovis, California. We gathered GIS data, applied it to our 
Route Optimization tool, and created an export to FleetMind.  
Univerus Routing is confident that it can provide a solution that meets or exceeds the needs 
described in this RFP, and that our experience working with cities like Cheyenne will allow the 
project run smoothly and successfully. 
Should you have any questions regarding our proposal or would like to schedule a demonstration of 
our solution, please reach out to Gus Esposito – Sales Manager, contact details provided on the 
cover page of proposal.   
We look forward to hearing from you regarding next steps. 
 
 
Todd Ohman - EVP Univerus North America

MEMORANDUM

 ______________________________________________ 
Three (3) Responsive Proposals were submitted under Request for Proposal (RFP) #8-24, for the 
Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project. 
  
The proposals were received from:  
1. Landmark Aquatics 
2. TDSI, Inc. 
3. Stockwell Engineers 
 
Upon review of the proposals by the evaluation panel, the group has recommended acceptance of 
the proposal submitted by Landmark Aquatics, for a project award amount of $236,957.00 
 
Basis for award was based on a Qualifications and Cost based review and scoring of proposals. 
 
Funding:   
 032-17-1721-45-43113 (SPOT 2021 Ballot / Johnson Pool Project)

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for 
JOHNSON POOL EVALUATION AND DESIGN 
 
This Professional Services Agreement (“Agreement”) is made and entered into by and 
between the City of Cheyenne, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
(“City”), and Landmark Aquatic, located at 505 Violet Street, Golden, Colorado 80401 
(“Consultant”).  This Agreement shall become effective upon the date of the last signature 
affixed hereto. 
 
RECITALS 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by 
and between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the Johnson Pool Evaluation 
and Design Project, in accordance with City of Cheyenne Request for Proposal (RFP) 
#8-24, incorporated herein by reference, and the Consultant’s submitted proposal, 
incorporated herein by reference. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 
The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed by October 3, 2024. 
 
3. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the 
Consultant will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant Two Hundred Fifteen 
Thousand Dollars ($215,000.00) for the base scope, Twenty-One Thousand, Nine 
Hundred Fifty-Seven Dollars ($21,957.00) for the alternate roof structure scope, for 
a total not to exceed Two Hundred Thirty-Six Thousand, Nine Hundred Fifty-Seven 
Dollars ($236,957.00), as stated in Exhibit A – Price Proposal. 
 
4. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances. Upon notice to the 
Consultant, the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services 
not meeting such a standard. 
 
5. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement 
in accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances.

6. 
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug-Free Workplace Act of November 1988, the City of 
Cheyenne has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to 
alcohol and drug usage by City Employees. All architects, engineers, and other 
consultants under contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and 
sub-consultants, are required to comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and 
Controlled Substance Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites 
occupied by the Consultant while performing the duties and responsibilities of the 
Agreement. It is the responsibility of the Consultant to familiarize themselves with the 
requirements of this policy and to inform all their employees and sub-consultants of the 
requirements and ensure their compliance. If the Consultant, their employees, or 
sub-consultants are found in violation of this policy, the Agreement may be terminated. 
 
7. 
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS 
The Consultant agrees to indemnify, hold harmless, and defend the City from and against 
any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures, and suits, and the cost and expenses 
incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a 
result the performance of the Consultant’s duties, including death or bodily injury to any 
person, destruction or damage to any property, contamination of or adverse effects on the 
environment, or any violation of governmental laws, regulations, or orders to the extent 
caused by (1) the Consultant’s breach of any term or provision of this Agreement; or (2) 
any negligent or wrongful act, error, or omission by the Consultant, or its employees or 
sub-consultants in the performance of this Agreement.  The Consultant acknowledges 
that it may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant to the terms of this paragraph. 
 
8. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Consultant must provide proof of the following insurance coverages: 
 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness, or death, or from damage to or destruction 
of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of $1,000,000 
per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the Agreement, 
as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services under this 
Agreement.  Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’ compensation or 
employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
 
Professional Liability Insurance 
The Consultant shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City from 
any and all claims arising from the Consultant’s negligence in the performance of duties 
under this Agreement.  The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be 
provided pursuant to an “occurrence” policy. 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 8-24 
Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project  
Page 3 of 7 
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
 
 
a. 
Consultant shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, 
provide the City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such 
coverage is or shall be in effect at the time the Consultant begins the 
provision of services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
b. 
In the event the Consultant’s services extend into a future policy period, 
the Consultant shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City 
with a new certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or 
shall be in effect during all periods of time that Consultant will provide 
services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
c. 
Consultant shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five 
(5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services 
under this Agreement; and 
 
 
d. 
In the event the Consultant or the insurer terminates “claims made” 
coverage prior to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs 
a., b., or c. of this paragraph, the Consultant shall provide to the City 
advance written notification of the termination of said coverage and shall 
provide the City with an endorsement for an extended reporting period 
(“tail coverage”) which shall be in effect for a period of time not less than 
five (5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services 
under this Agreement.  
 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
 
The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by 
endorsement on its insurance policies and shall provide the City with a copy of the 
endorsements.  This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and 
professional liability insurance policies. 
 
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the 
above-stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All 
policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the 
Agreement.  It shall be an affirmative obligation upon Consultant to immediately notify 
in writing the city risk manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or 
occurrence that has resulted in or may result in the cancellation of, or substantive change 
to any insurance coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be 
construed to be a breach of this Agreement. 
 
If requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of insurance 
policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional insured.  
The City’s failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall 
not affect the City’s rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder.

Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a minimum

12. 
TAXES 
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, 
debts, and obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this 
Agreement. 
 
13. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
14. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default.  In the event the default 
remains uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
 
 
a. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not 
exceed the contract amount; or 
 
 
b. 
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
15. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
The term of this Agreement shall be from date of execution to October 3, 2024.  The 
parties may by mutual written agreement renew or extend this Agreement. 
 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 8-24 
Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project  
Page 5 of 7 
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Consultant, 
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. 
Upon receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall: 
 
 
a. 
Discontinue all services affected; and 
 
 
b. 
Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications, 
reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as 
may have been accumulated by the Consultant in performing this 
Agreement, whether completed or in process. 
 
 
c. 
In the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the 
Consultant for accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of 
termination. Such acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
 
16. 
ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, 
the City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Consultant. In the event of 
default of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default 
to commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear 
its own costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.  
 
17. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE

seq., and all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law,

MEMO 
 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins, City of Cheyenne 
Members of the City Council, City of Cheyenne 
 
FROM: 
Erin Gates 
 
DATE: 
May 8, 2024 
 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration of RFP #9-24 for a Professional Service Agreement between the 
City of Cheyenne and TPT Trails, LLC design and construction services for the 
Belvoir Ranch Phase 2, Design-Build Trail project.  
 
 
Proposals were solicited for the Belvoir Ranch Phase 2, Design-Build project. This project is for 
the design services and construction of approximately 6.5 miles of a single-track trail system for 
multi-use, nonmotorized use. One written technical proposal, with a sealed price proposal, was 
received from TPT Trails, LLC. 
 
A review committee comprised of City Staff from Public Works, Community Recreation and 
Events, Planning and Development, 1% Construction, and Engineering evaluated the submitted 
proposal for a qualification-based selection. The price proposal was opened following the ranking 
of the submitted proposal. The price proposal appears appropriate for the scope of services 
described in the Request for Proposals and the submitted written technical proposal. 
 
The fees include services design, construction, and GIS data for this project. Funding for this 
project will come from the Belvoir Recreation Fund, GL 015-21-2111-45-21301. The total 
professional and construction services will be a cost not-to-exceed $219,672.93. We respectfully 
request your approval of this professional services agreement.

2101 O’Neil Avenue

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for 
Belvoir Ranch Phase 2, Design-Build Trail 
 
This Professional Services Agreement (“Agreement”) is made and entered into by and 
between the City of Cheyenne, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
(“City”), and TPT Trails, LLC, located at 701 W. 30th Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming 
82001(“Consultant”). This Agreement shall become effective upon the date of the last signature 
affixed hereto. 
 
RECITALS 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by 
and between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the above-mentioned project, 
in accordance with the Scope of Work attached hereto as Exhibit A, incorporated by 
reference and expressly made a part hereof. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 
The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed by November 30, 2024, as 
stated in Exhibit A. 
 
3. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the 
Consultant will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant a not to exceed amount 
of two-hundred nineteen thousand, six-hundred seventy-two dollars, and ninety-three 
cents ($219,672.93) as stated in Exhibit B. 
 
4. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances. Upon notice to the 
Consultant, the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services 
not meeting such a standard. 
 
5. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement 
in accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
 
 
10:14 am, May 07 2024

6. 
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug-Free Workplace Act of November 1988, the City of 
Cheyenne has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to 
alcohol and drug usage by City Employees. All architects, engineers, and other 
consultants under contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and 
sub-consultants, are required to comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and 
Controlled Substance Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites 
occupied by the Consultant while performing the duties and responsibilities of the 
Agreement. It is the responsibility of the Consultant to familiarize themselves with the 
requirements of this policy and to inform all their employees and sub-consultants of the 
requirements and ensure their compliance. If the Consultant, their employees or 
sub-consultants are found in violation of this policy, the Agreement may be terminated. 
 
7.

any negligent or wrongful act, error, or omission by the Consultant, or its employees or 
sub-consultants in the performance of this Agreement. The Consultant acknowledges that 
it may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant to the terms of this paragraph. 
 
8.

Workers’ Compensation

as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services under this 
Agreement. Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’ compensation or

under this Agreement.  The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be

REMEDIES
